### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-001

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01

MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The instruments used during the greenhouse experiment _____ calibrated before each trial.

CHOICES:
[
  "was",
  "being",
  "were",
  "has been"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The plural subject “instruments” takes the plural auxiliary “were.”

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-002

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 159 examples from 8 settings over 6 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-005

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01

DIFFICULTY: easy

PROMPT:
The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 162 examples from 3 settings over 5 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supports",
  "supporting",
  "have supported"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-006

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 163 examples from 4 settings over 7 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-009

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 166 examples from 7 settings over 6 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 169 examples from 10 settings over 5 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-013

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation.

Passage 2: An account of whether an imaging method detects canopy changes more consistently after calibration becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers compared records from several communities rather than treating one locality as representative of the whole region. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 170 examples from 3 settings over 7 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

PROMPT:
Study focus: a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 173 examples from 6 settings over 6 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-017

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 174 examples from 7 settings over 8 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-018

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Several examples show the same broad tendency, although the feature serves a different role in each setting. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 175 examples from 8 settings over 10 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. An ordinary detail is placed beside a later reflection so that the reader can infer a change in the character’s perspective. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 177 examples from 10 settings over 7 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-021

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Researchers considered differences among local conditions before drawing a broader conclusion about the policy. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 178 examples from 3 settings over 9 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-022

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 179 examples from 4 settings over 4 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-023

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest.

Passage 2: In examining farmers adapting cooperative practices when regional prices became less predictable, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis separated the broad pattern from the possibility that one unusually large reading affected the first comparison. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 180 examples from 5 settings over 6 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-024

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character notices a small change in the setting and considers whether it signals a larger change in circumstances. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 181 examples from 6 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-025

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 182 examples from 7 settings over 10 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supports",
  "supporting",
  "have supported"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-026

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 183 examples from 8 settings over 5 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-027

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-1
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 184 examples from 9 settings over 7 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-028

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. An object that first appears incidental becomes relevant when the character considers what has changed around it. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 185 examples from 10 settings over 9 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-029

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 186 examples from 3 settings over 4 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-030

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Several examples show the same broad tendency, although the feature serves a different role in each setting. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 187 examples from 4 settings over 6 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-031

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The final analysis treated the direction of the trend and the size of the effect as related but distinct questions. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 188 examples from 5 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-032

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2

SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 189 examples from 6 settings over 10 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-033

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition.

Passage 2: In examining the movement of sediment after vegetation is restored along a riverbank, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. Researchers considered differences among local conditions before drawing a broader conclusion about the policy. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 190 examples from 7 settings over 5 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-034

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A broader comparison shows that the recurring feature can serve more than one function. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 191 examples from 8 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

STATUS: LEGACY

  "supporting",
  "have supported",

The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-036

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01

SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2

DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the restoration choices made when a damaged architectural feature has several plausible originals. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 193 examples from 10 settings over 4 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-037

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 194 examples from 3 settings over 6 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-038

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers examined the object alongside related examples from the same period before drawing a conclusion about its purpose. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 195 examples from 4 settings over 8 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-039

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 196 examples from 5 settings over 10 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-040

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. An object that first appears incidental becomes relevant when the character considers what has changed around it. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 197 examples from 6 settings over 5 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-041

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The evidence combines formal policy records with descriptions of how residents actually used the new arrangement. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 198 examples from 7 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-042

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 199 examples from 8 settings over 9 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-043

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation.

Passage 2: An account of a policy experiment designed to reduce congestion without restricting access to businesses becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The final analysis treated the direction of the trend and the size of the effect as related but distinct questions. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 200 examples from 9 settings over 4 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-044

STATUS: LEGACY

DIFFICULTY: medium

An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. An ordinary detail is placed beside a later reflection so that the reader can infer a change in the character’s perspective. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 201 examples from 10 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-045

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 202 examples from 3 settings over 8 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supports",
  "supporting",
  "have supported"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-046

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the changing use of color in a regional school of landscape painting. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 203 examples from 4 settings over 10 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-047

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 204 examples from 5 settings over 5 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-048

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character notices a small change in the setting and considers whether it signals a larger change in circumstances. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 205 examples from 6 settings over 7 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-049

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 206 examples from 7 settings over 9 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-050

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers examined the object alongside related examples from the same period before drawing a conclusion about its purpose. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 207 examples from 8 settings over 4 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-051

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The researchers considered both the average response and the spread of individual observations before drawing the broader conclusion. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 208 examples from 9 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-052

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01

MODULE: rw-module-2

SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 209 examples from 10 settings over 8 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-053

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The evidence suggests that the change was not caused by a single decision but developed through several local responses. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest.

Passage 2: In examining how migratory birds alter stopover timing when food availability changes, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The evidence combines formal policy records with descriptions of how residents actually used the new arrangement. The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 210 examples from 3 settings over 10 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-054

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The comparison indicates that a feature often described as decorative also served a practical communicative purpose. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. Several examples show the same broad tendency, although the feature serves a different role in each setting. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 211 examples from 4 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

Study focus: the acoustic design of small performance spaces. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 213 examples from 6 settings over 9 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-057

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Contemporary records show that the policy produced a visible change, although the effect varied across neighborhoods. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 214 examples from 7 settings over 4 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "qualify",
  "decorate",
  "separate"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-058

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A broader comparison shows that the recurring feature can serve more than one function. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 215 examples from 8 settings over 6 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the account proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the account treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the account identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the account focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-059

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 216 examples from 9 settings over 8 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,",
  "However,"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-060

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining a student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. The narrator initially treats the change as an inconvenience, but a later observation makes the earlier judgment seem incomplete. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition. The character notices a small change in the setting and considers whether it signals a larger change in circumstances. The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 217 examples from 10 settings over 10 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-061

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. Researchers compared records from several communities rather than treating one locality as representative of the whole region. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 218 examples from 3 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-062

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Boundaries
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 219 examples from 4 settings over 7 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; however,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-063

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
In examining how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Measurements show a consistent overall pattern, but the magnitude of the effect changes under different conditions. This distinction matters because the evidence describes what occurred under the observed conditions, not every possible condition.

Passage 2: In examining changes in how local newspapers described public libraries in the early twentieth century, researchers focused on what changed rather than assuming that the change had a single explanation. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The researchers considered both the average response and the spread of individual observations before drawing the broader conclusion. The analysis of how seedlings respond to intermittent rather than constant light drew on 220 examples from 5 settings over 9 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-064

STATUS: LEGACY

A recent study of a musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The scene moves from a concrete detail to a reflection that reveals the character’s uncertainty rather than resolving it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. An object that first appears incidental becomes relevant when the character considers what has changed around it. The analysis of musician deciding whether to preserve a family composition unchanged drew on 221 examples from 6 settings over 4 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-065

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: standard-english-conventions
SKILL: Form, Structure, and Sense
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of community debate over whether a new transit route should follow an older commercial corridor drew on 222 examples from 7 settings over 6 observation periods. The set of measurements, rather than the individual readings, _____ the basis for comparison.

CHOICES:
[
  "support",
  "supports",
  "supporting",
  "have supported"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-066

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Rhetorical Synthesis
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
Study focus: the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth. Observation: the evidence shows a measurable difference between the two settings. Qualification: the size of the difference depends on conditions surrounding the observation. Goal: state the result accurately without making a broader claim than the evidence supports. The analysis of linguist comparing how speakers signal uncertainty in two related dialects drew on 223 examples from 8 settings over 8 observation periods. The comparison was reviewed again so that the result could be stated without dropping its important qualification.

The student wants to emphasize the study’s main result while preserving its important qualification. Which choice best accomplishes this goal?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations and recorded them at several locations.",
  "The study examined a defined group during a specified period.",
  "The comparison reveals a measurable effect, although its size depends on the conditions studied.",
  "The project used multiple observations but did not report a result."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-067

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Words in Context
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. A follow-up measurement supports the broad interpretation while identifying a condition that limits how widely it can be applied. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. The analysis of the effect of water temperature on the activity of a freshwater organism drew on 224 examples from 9 settings over 10 observation periods.

As used in the text, what does “qualify” most nearly mean?

CHOICES:
[
  "measure",
  "decorate",
  "separate",
  "qualify"
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
In context, qualify means to limit or modify a statement so that it is not interpreted too broadly.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-068

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Central Ideas and Details
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The character values continuity, yet the setting repeatedly introduces evidence that change has already occurred. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. An ordinary detail is placed beside a later reflection so that the reader can infer a change in the character’s perspective. The analysis of traveler recognizing a familiar landscape from an unfamiliar direction drew on 225 examples from 10 settings over 5 observation periods.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The the passage identifies a pattern while qualifying how broadly it should be interpreted.",
  "The the passage proves that the observed result occurs in every comparable setting.",
  "The the passage treats one observation as sufficient to establish a universal rule.",
  "The the passage focuses mainly on background details without reaching an interpretive conclusion."
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-069

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: expression-of-ideas
SKILL: Transitions
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
The analysis of municipal efforts to coordinate street markets as cities expanded drew on 226 examples from 3 settings over 7 observation periods. The researchers observed a strong overall pattern. _____, the size of the effect differed among the study conditions.

CHOICES:
[
  "For example,",
  "However,",
  "Similarly,",
  "In addition,"
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
However correctly signals the contrast between the broad pattern and the difference in effect size.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-070

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Text Structure and Purpose
DIFFICULTY: easy
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. The evidence becomes more informative when the object is considered alongside the setting in which audiences encountered it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation. A broader comparison shows that the recurring feature can serve more than one function. The analysis of how archaeologists infer trade connections from recurring ceramic materials drew on 227 examples from 4 settings over 9 observation periods.

Why does the author include the information about the conditions surrounding the result?

CHOICES:
[
  "To replace the central finding with an unrelated explanation.",
  "To provide background that has no effect on the interpretation.",
  "To qualify a broader finding by identifying a condition that limits it.",
  "To repeat the earlier observation without adding information."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-071

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Inferences
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. The analysis separated the broad pattern from the possibility that one unusually large reading affected the first comparison. The analysis of whether a coating changes the stability of a battery electrode during repeated cycles drew on 228 examples from 5 settings over 4 observation periods.

Which inference is best supported by the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The observed relationship must remain identical regardless of surrounding conditions.",
  "The evidence establishes a single cause with no need for further comparison.",
  "The observations show that the measured difference is too small to interpret.",
  "The observed relationship may change when relevant surrounding conditions change."
]

ANSWER: D

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-072

STATUS: LEGACY

The analysis of student sorting letters left by a relative who avoided public attention drew on 229 examples from 6 settings over 6 observation periods. The revised method produced a clearer signal _____ it required additional calibration.

CHOICES:
[
  "; however,",
  "; therefore,",
  "; for example,",
  "; similarly,"
]

ANSWER: A

EXPLANATION:
The semicolon separates two independent clauses, while however correctly signals their contrast.

SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: craft-and-structure
SKILL: Cross-Text Connections
DIFFICULTY: hard
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
A recent study of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth compared observations from different conditions instead of treating the first pattern as conclusive. Researchers distinguish the stated purpose of the initiative from the later uses residents found for it. Taken together, the observations support a useful pattern but leave room for a more qualified interpretation.

Passage 2: An account of whether an imaging method detects canopy changes more consistently after calibration becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The experiment separates an observed association from the stronger claim that one factor alone caused the outcome. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers compared records from several communities rather than treating one locality as representative of the whole region. The analysis of the spread of neighborhood associations during a period of rapid population growth drew on 230 examples from 7 settings over 8 observation periods.

Based on the two passages, which statement would both authors most likely agree with?

CHOICES:
[
  "Both authors argue that a result must be identical in every setting.",
  "Both authors support a qualified interpretation of a broad pattern.",
  "Both authors conclude that local conditions make comparison unnecessary.",
  "Both authors treat a single observation as sufficient for a universal rule."
]

ANSWER: B

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

### Question: sat-series-a-mock-01-rw-074

STATUS: LEGACY
TEST KEY: sat-series-a-mock-01
TEST ID: sat-series-a-mock-01
SECTION: reading-writing
MODULE: rw-module-2
DOMAIN: information-and-ideas
SKILL: Command of Evidence
DIFFICULTY: medium
QUESTION TYPE: multiple-choice

PROMPT:
An account of a museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes becomes more informative when its evidence is considered alongside the circumstances in which it was collected. The researchers caution that a recurring feature does not by itself establish a single origin or intention. The result therefore supports a narrower claim than a simple comparison might initially suggest. Researchers examined the object alongside related examples from the same period before drawing a conclusion about its purpose. The analysis of museum study of how visitors move between objects with related themes drew on 231 examples from 8 settings over 10 observation periods.

Which finding would best support the interpretation presented in the text?

CHOICES:
[
  "The researchers collected observations from more than one location or setting.",
  "The researchers recorded both averages and individual observations.",
  "A follow-up observation reproduces the predicted pattern after the relevant condition is changed.",
  "The study followed earlier work on a related question."
]

ANSWER: C

EXPLANATION:
The first choice directly reflects the evidence relationship and cognitive task specified by the construction blueprint.

STATUS: LEGACY

CHOICES:

  "supporting",
  "have supported",

The singular subject set takes the singular verb supports.
